The TIP is stated for "the year you paid the tax", which is 2024 only when the refund is for 2024 and was paid then, so items 1 and 5 can make a refund taxable even when you did not itemize for 2024, and a part of the refund for a 2025 payment changes the 2025 deduction for taxes (Pub. 525 p. 24, Example 28). Items 3, 4, 6, 7, 8 and 9 and the nonresident rule change only how much of a refund of a deducted tax is included; when no income tax was deducted for 2024, none is (Pub. 525 p. 24, Deductions not itemized). Box 8 sends a taxable refund to Schedule C or F whatever the itemizing. A recovery deducted on an earlier Form 8960 is refused by the existing screen f8960.refund, asked of every return. A refund of the sales tax itself is a different recovery, refused (refund.other). (i1040gi, p. 88, Line 1, "None of your refund is taxable if, in the year you paid the tax"; p525 p. 24, State tax refund, "Because you didn’t choose to deduct the state income tax, you don’t include the state income tax refund in income")

== Form 4562, Part I — Election To Expense Certain Property Under Section 179 (Summary for the return) ==
  2   Total cost of section 179 property placed in service
      = $0
      cite: [i4562] p. 4, Line 2, including "Any property placed in service by your spouse" (2025; 5c1c95b7)
      note: confirmed roster $person: taxpayer
  (helper) The maximum section 179 deduction limitation for 2025
      = $2,500,000
      cite: [i4562] p. 4, Worksheet 1, line 2, "$2,500,000" (2025; 5c1c95b7)
  1   Maximum amount
      = $2,500,000
      cite: [i4562] p. 4, Line 1, "Generally, the maximum section 179 expense deduction is $2,500,000 for section 179 property ... placed in service during the tax year beginning in 2025" (2025; 5c1c95b7)
      note: Interpretation amb.2025.s179_carryover_no_property: The second. s179.line1 is the $2,500,000 the line 1 instruction states, so line 5 is the dollar limitation and a carryover is deducted up to the dollar limitation less the 2025 election and up to the business income limit (line 11). On a separate return line 5 is 50% of the limitation, the instruction's allocation when the spouses elect no other. Research for the owner (ambiguities/research/amb.2025.s179_carryover_no_property.md) quotes the archived 26 U.S.C. 179(b)(3)(B), Treas. Reg. 1.179-2(b) and 1.179-3(b) to (d), and P.L. 119-21 sec. 70306. The carryover's room is "the limitation of paragraphs (1) and (2)", the dollar amount, which the cost of 2025 property reduces only above $4,000,000. Form 4562 computes exactly that when line 1 is the $2,500,000 the line 1 instruction states (p. 4). The optional Worksheet 1 instead takes the smaller of that and the cost of 2025 property, a cap that changes no return without a carryover and departs from the statute on a return with one. (i4562, p. 4, Line 1 and Worksheet 1; p. 5, Line 5)
  3   Threshold cost of section 179 property before reduction in limitation
      = $4,000,000
      cite: [i4562] p. 4, Worksheet 1, line 6, "$4,000,000" (2025; 5c1c95b7)
  4   Reduction in limitation. Subtract line 3 from line 2. If zero or less, enter -0-
      = $0
      cite: [f4562] Form 4562 (2025), line 4 (2025; created 2025-10-09; c05f9d1f)
  (helper) Subtract line 4 from line 1. If zero or less, enter -0-
      = $2,500,000
      cite: [f4562] Form 4562 (2025), line 5 (2025; created 2025-10-09; c05f9d1f)
  (helper) Married filing separately
      = false
      cite: [i4562] p. 5, Line 5, "If you are married filing separately" (2025; 5c1c95b7)
  (helper) On a separate return, that limitation multiplied by 50%

Interpretation amb.2025.box2c_scope: Refuse nonzero box 2c; no exclusion or AMT amount is silently omitted. Archived Schedule D instructions require the exclusion on Form 8949 code Q and describe an AMT adjustment for 50/60/75-percent exclusions. The existing explicit exclusions are retained; the owner was notified and asked, then said continue without selecting an expansion. (i1040sd, p. 8, Gain from Form 1099-DIV and Alternative Minimum Tax)
Interpretation amb.2025.filer_under_16: The second. The refusal cites the one sentence the text has about age 16 and names the register entry; it does not claim the text forbids the return. The owner's decision of 2026-10-22 (integrity plan decision 10). A return for a child under 16 is one the tool's slice does not model well (Form 8615 is refused through scope.other_line16 only when the filer knows to answer it); the refusal makes the age an explicit entry rather than a silent assumption. (i1040gi, p. 66, Self-Select PIN, "You can’t use the Self-Select PIN method if you are a first-time filer under age 16 at the end of 2025")
Interpretation amb.2025.alimony_screen: The first (s1.alimony, s1.alimony_unsupported; amounts under an agreement entered into after 2018, or after a change that expressly excludes them, are not asked, as the questions say, and are neither income nor deductible). The owner's decision (Stage 16 decision batch, D2, with the screens of the premise map, R-ALI-1 and R-ALI-2). The statement listing the other agreements and recipients, and the recapture worksheet, are not prepared; a payment partly child support needs the Pub. 504 allocation rules. (p504, p. 12, Alimony, "If you paid alimony to more than one person, enter the SSN or ITIN of one of the recipients"; p. 16, Recapture of Alimony; p. 13, Underpayment)
Interpretation amb.2025.cod_fully_taxable: The first (s1.cod_amount, s1.cod_unsupported). Student loans are refused with the other exceptions. The owner's decision (Stage 16 decision batch, D2, "fully taxable canceled nonbusiness debt"). An excepted student loan discharge is nontaxable and usually comes with no Form 1099-C (p. 3, TIP), so a filer who reports one is refused rather than told how much of it to include; the conservative choice where D2 is silent. (p4681, p. 3, General Rules, "There are several exceptions and exclusions that may result in part or all of a canceled debt being nontaxable"; p. 4, Exceptions)
Interpretation amb.2025.ewp_oid: The first (interest.penalty, totaled on s1.line18 and copied to Form 8960 line 10). The owner's decision (Stage 16 decision batch, D2, as corrected by the verification, E4) allows an input or a screen; the instruction treats the two forms alike, so an input loses nothing. Form 1099-OID is not archived, so its box number is not named. (i1040gi, p. 95, Line 18, "The Form 1099-INT or Form 1099-OID you received will show the amount of any penalty you were charged")
Interpretation amb.2025.jury_24a_cap: The first (s1.jury_over refuses s1.line24a). The pay given to the employer is part of the pay received (p525 p. 34, "you can deduct the amount turned over to your employer"), so a larger amount is an entry error to correct, not a case to cap silently; as with w2.g_bad, the refusal fires only on an impossible entry. Premise map Q14, left open by D3. (i1040gi, p. 99, Line 24a, "Enter your jury duty pay if you gave the pay to your employer because your employer paid your salary while you served on the jury")
Interpretation amb.2025.sa_medical_negative_agi: The second (sa.agi_floor, copied to line 2). The instruction names a part of the expenses, and no part of the expenses exceeds the expenses themselves; the signed reading would deduct $324.85 of medical expenses on a return with none (engine differential case se-00255, BUG-0094). Tax-Calculator (calcfunctions.ItemDed, posagi = max(c00100, 0.)) and PolicyEngine-US both floor adjusted gross income at zero before taking the percentage. Equivalent to flooring line 3 at zero; the floor is placed on line 2's operand so that lines 3 and 4 keep their printed wording. (i1040sca, p. 1, Medical and Dental Expenses, "You can deduct only the part of your medical and dental expenses that exceeds 7.5% of the amount of your adjusted gross income on Form 1040 or 1040-SR, line 11b")
Interpretation amb.2025.multiplication_subcent: Round the product half-up to the nearest cent at the multiplication. Matches the half-up convention of the printed whole-dollar rounding rules. The whole-dollar default is unaffected: whole-dollar amounts multiplied by these rates produce exact cents, so the reading only matters when cents are turned on.
 (i1040gi, QDCGT worksheet, lines 18 and 21; Rounding Off to Whole Dollars, p. 23)
Interpretation amb.2025.qbi_cooperative_patron: Refuse any patron of an agricultural or horticultural cooperative, specified or not (qbi.unsupported). The owner asked for the strictly accurate reading. Refusing every patron satisfies both texts: a filer the form excludes is never computed on it, and a filer the instructions exclude is a patron the form also excludes. The cost is a refusal for a patron of a cooperative that is not specified, which the instructions would allow.
 (f8995, Form 8995 (2025), note above line 1; i8995, Who Can Use Form 8995; i1040gi line 13a)
Interpretation amb.2025.sd4952_selection: Use the detailed worksheet conditions, including line 4g and exceptions. The worksheet explicitly limits its own use and the Form 1040 line 16 instructions repeat the line 4g condition. (i1040sd, p. 15, Schedule D Tax Worksheet, applicability; compare f1040sd line 20 and i1040gi p. 36)
Interpretation amb.2025.savers_traditional: The first (person.sav_line1 adds person.ira_contrib). The owner's decision (Stage 16 decision 10). The line names traditional IRA contributions without limiting them to the deductible ones. Excess or recharacterized contributions, traditional or Roth, are refused by one screen per person (person.ira_excess), which also answers Stage 15 owner question 4. (f8880, Form 8880 (2025), line 1, "Traditional and Roth IRA contributions")
Interpretation amb.2025.clwb_claiming_part1: The first (f5695.claiming_part1). The second is circular (line 15 depends on whether Worksheet B is completed). The readings cannot differ in any amount. With Worksheet B completed, the Part I limit subtracts Worksheet B line 14, which is never more than Schedule 8812 line 12, instead of Form 1040 line 19, the smaller of line 12 and Credit Limit Worksheet A line 3; so the limit with Worksheet B is at least the limit without it. If line 13 is more than zero and line 15 is zero with Worksheet B, it is zero without it too, and Credit Limit Worksheet A line 4 (Worksheet B line 15, Schedule 3 line 5a) is zero either way; if line 15 is more than zero, both readings complete Worksheet B. (i1040s8812, p. 4, Credit Limit Worksheet A, "Complete Credit Limit Worksheet B only if you meet all the following")
Interpretation amb.2025.dc_ineligible_providers: The first (dcprov.related asks Pub. 503's list; a Yes refuses Form 2441). The owner's decision (Stage 15, decision 6). The publication is the more specific text, and both lists agree on a dependent and a child under 19. A provider in either list refuses, so the reading decides only which question is asked. (p503, p. 8, Payments to Relatives or Dependents, "However, don't count any amounts you pay to")
Interpretation amb.2025.dc_line30: The first (f2441.line30; dependent.dc_paid and dependent.dc_line2d ask and show each person's expenses including those paid with dependent care benefits). The owner's decision (Stage 15, decision 6). The total is determined by the text and verifiable; an allocation of line 28 among persons is not. In Pub. 503's example (the Paris family, p. 13) the benefits covered one child's expenses and the total less the benefits is the line 30 the example uses ($19,500 less $1,000). (f2441, Form 2441 (2025), line 30)
Interpretation amb.2025.dc_line1e_attribution: The first (person.dc_1e, f2441.attrib_gate). On a return that is not joint there is one person and line 1e is theirs. When the split is undetermined but no credit remains (line 31 is zero, so line 3 and line 6 are zero whatever lines 4 and 5 hold), line 1e is shown in the taxpayer's line 4, keeping lines 4 and 5 together equal to line 1z plus self-employment earnings; the credit cannot differ. The owner's decision (Stage 15, decision 6). Taxable benefits arise from one spouse's plan; the instructions give no rule for dividing a joint line 26, and the case where it matters (benefits over the exclusion with a credit still left) is rare, since excluded benefits of $5,000 leave at most $1,000 of the $6,000 limit. (i2441, p. 4, Lines 4 and 5, "If filing jointly, figure your and your spouse's earned income separately")
Interpretation amb.2025.dc_rate_nonpositive_agi: The first (tables/2025/dc_rate.yaml, the first band open below). The owner's ruling. In this tool the readings cannot differ in the credit. Line 7 (adjusted gross income) of zero or less gives taxable income of zero and no tax on Form 1040 line 16, and Schedule 2 Part I (line 17) is zero here, the alternative minimum tax and the repayment of excess advance premium tax credit being refused (s2.amt and the Schedule 2 screen); so line 18 is zero, and the Credit Limit Worksheet's line 3 and line 10 are zero. A rate is still needed for line 8 to be known. (f2441, Form 2441 (2025), line 8)
Interpretation amb.2025.dc_plan_maximum: The first (f2441.plan_max, f2441.plans_differ). The owner's decision (Stage 15, decision 6). Where every plan allows the same maximum the readings agree; where they differ the text does not decide, so that case is refused. Interpretation amb.2025.f5695_battery_second_home: The first (f5695.line5a asks for battery storage technology installed in connection with your home in the United States, not your main home). The owner's decision. The text names the main home where it means it (fuel cell property, line 7a) and does not for the battery; the Part I general rule is that improvements to "more than one home that you used as a residence" go on one Form 5695 (i5695 p. 2). (i5695, p. 2, Qualified battery storage technology costs, "installed in connection with your home located in the United States")
Interpretation amb.2025.f5695_17abc_section_a: The first (f5695.sec_a_claimed gates lines 18b, 19h and 20d only). The owner's decision. Section B has its own residence test on line 21a, "a home located in the United States that you use as a residence", which is not limited to the main home, and its own original-use test on line 21b, and the audit has its own test on line 26a; the form's direction is "Do not complete Part II, Section A". Reading the No as disallowing Section B would make line 21a's wider residence test unreachable for anyone without Section A improvements on a main home. (f5695, Form 5695 (2025), lines 17a to 17c and 21a; i5695 p. 5, Lines 17a Through 17e)
Interpretation amb.2025.f5695_section_b_residence: The first (f5695.line21a's question says the home may be a second home or a home you rent). The owner's decision. Line 21a and the Section B paragraph state the wider test, line 21c asks for "each home", and Fact Sheet 2025-01 says "such property may be installed in the taxpayer’s second home" and that "the taxpayer may rent the home". The main-home statements are in Section A (lines 17a and 17d) and in a summary in the Form 1040 instructions. (fs-2025-01, p. 5, Energy Efficient Home Improvement Credit - Qualifying residence, "In this case, the taxpayer may rent the home in which such property is installed, and such property may be installed in the taxpayer’s second home")
Interpretation amb.2025.f5695_category_cap: The first (f5695.ac_capped, wh_capped, furnace_capped and enabling_capped against f5695.item_max). The owner's decision to encode the form as printed. Interpretation amb.2025.savers_ineligible_column: The first (f8880.line6a and f8880.line6b through f8880.elig_a and f8880.elig_b). The owner's decision (Stage 15 premise map, decision 4). The test is of "the person(s) who made the qualified contribution or elective deferral", so it reads as a test of each contributor, and the form keeps each spouse's contributions in their own column. Whether a person is claimed as a dependent is asked of each person (person.sav_claimed), not read from the return's "can claim" question; it is asked only when someone can claim you or your spouse (sa.dependent), since otherwise no one is claimed. (f8880, p. 2, Who Can Take This Credit, "The person(s) who made the qualified contribution or elective deferral (a) was born after January 1, 2008; (b) is claimed as a dependent on someone else’s 2025 tax return; or (c) was a student")
Interpretation amb.2025.hoh_community_property: The second, for the nine states the instructions list (Direct File''s knockout tests eight; Louisiana is in the text and so in the question). The archived text states the community income rule but not a prohibition on the filing status; the refusal rests on the rule being outside the tool, not on a forbidden status. Interpretation amb.2025.ira_se_earned: The first. Interpretation amb.2025.refund_exceptions: The first (refund.box8 and refund.not_2024 on refund.taxable; refund.exc_tax and refund.exc_who on refund.line1; refund.other on Schedule 1 line 1). The TIP is stated for "the year you paid the tax", which is 2024 only when the refund is for 2024 and was paid then, so items 1 and 5 can make a refund taxable even when you did not itemize for 2024, and a part of the refund for a 2025 payment changes the 2025 deduction for taxes (Pub. 525 p. 24, Example 28). Items 3, 4, 6, 7, 8 and 9 and the nonresident rule change only how much of a refund of a deducted tax is included; when no income tax was deducted for 2024, none is (Pub. 525 p. 24, Deductions not itemized). Box 8 sends a taxable refund to Schedule C or F whatever the itemizing. A recovery deducted on an earlier Form 8960 is refused by the existing screen f8960.refund, asked of every return. (i1040gi, pp. 88–89, Line 1, "Exception. See Itemized Deduction Recoveries in Pub. 525 instead of using the State and Local Income Tax Refund Worksheet"; p525 p. 24, Deductions not itemized)
Interpretation amb.2025.refund_not_itemized: The first (refund.worksheet, refund.taxable). The owner's decision D5, with the verification's correction E5 of the premise map. The cap in Pub. 525 limits the refund of the tax you chose to deduct; for an income tax refund when sales tax was deducted, Pub. 525 p. 24, Example 27, says "Because you didn’t choose to deduct the state income tax, you don’t include the state income tax refund in income", agreeing with the TIP. A refund of the sales tax itself is a different recovery, refused (refund.other). (i1040gi, p. 88, Line 1, "None of your refund is taxable if, in the year you paid the tax"; p525 p. 24, State tax refund, "Because you didn’t choose to deduct the state income tax, you don’t include the state income tax refund in income")
Interpretation amb.2025.refund_paid_5d: The first (refund.line5d_2024). The owner's decision D6(b). The worksheet itself takes line 5d as the usual amount, and a filer reads it from the 2024 return. (i1040gi, p. 90, State and Local Income Tax Refund Worksheet—Schedule 1, Line 1, line 2, "(generally, this is the amount reported on your 2024 Schedule A, line 5d)")
Interpretation amb.2025.refund_cap_5d: The first (refund.line1). The owner's decision D6(a). The worksheet names line 5d. A refund larger than the income tax deducted on line 5a is exception 4 territory when sales taxes could have been deducted, and is caught there (refund.exc_tax). (i1040gi, p. 90, State and Local Income Tax Refund Worksheet—Schedule 1, Line 1, line 1, "But don’t enter more than the amount of your state and local income taxes shown on your 2024 Schedule A, line 5d")
Interpretation amb.2025.refund_boxes_2024: The first (refund.you_born, refund.you_blind, refund.spouse_born, refund.spouse_blind, refund.spouse_boxes). The owner's decision D6(c) (four yes/no questions, since a 2024 spouse may not be the 2025 one). Blindness at the end of the year and the spouse boxes for a joint or separate return follow the standard deduction instructions (i1040gi p. 33, Line 12d, for 2025, "If you or your spouse (if you are married and filing a joint return) were born before January 2, 1961, or were blind at the end of 2025" and "Don’t check any boxes for your spouse if your filing status is head of household"); a single or qualifying surviving spouse return has no spouse whose boxes it checks. The worksheet's "number of boxes checked" times the amount is written as the sum of the amount for each box checked (no atom makes a count from yes/no answers on a return-level form); the two are equal. (i1040gi, p. 90, State and Local Income Tax Refund Worksheet—Schedule 1, Line 1, line 6, "Check any boxes that apply"; p. 33, Line 12d, "Don’t check any boxes for your spouse if your filing status is head of household")
Interpretation amb.2025.s1_unnumbered_lines: The first (s1.line2b_notice, s1.line19b_notice, s1.line19c_notice, shown when line 2a or line 19a is more than zero). The owner's decision (Stage 16 decision batch, D12). No amount depends on them. Interpretation amb.2025.s179_carryover_no_property: The second. s179.line1 is the $2,500,000 the line 1 instruction states, so line 5 is the dollar limitation and a carryover is deducted up to the dollar limitation less the 2025 election and up to the business income limit (line 11). On a separate return line 5 is 50% of the limitation, the instruction's allocation when the spouses elect no other. Research for the owner (ambiguities/research/amb.2025.s179_carryover_no_property.md) quotes the archived 26 U.S.C. 179(b)(3)(B), Treas. Reg. 1.179-2(b) and 1.179-3(b) to (d), and P.L. 119-21 sec. 70306. The carryover's room is "the limitation of paragraphs (1) and (2)", the dollar amount, which the cost of 2025 property reduces only above $4,000,000. Form 4562 computes exactly that when line 1 is the $2,500,000 the line 1 instruction states (p. 4). The optional Worksheet 1 instead takes the smaller of that and the cost of 2025 property, a cap that changes no return without a carryover and departs from the statute on a return with one. (i4562, p. 4, Line 1 and Worksheet 1; p. 5, Line 5)
Interpretation amb.2025.gambling_losses: The first (sa.line16_gambling, sa.line16); a professional gambler (s1.gambling_pro) is refused at line 8b. The owner's decision D14 (Stage 16). The comparison of itemized and standard deductions (sa.itemizes) already applies the "only when itemizing" condition. Whether a professional gambler is asked only when there are winnings is the authoring agent's reading (a professional gambler with no winnings has nothing on line 8b or line 16). (i1040sca, p. 11, Other Itemized Deductions, "but only to the extent of gambling winnings reported on Schedule 1 (Form 1040), line 8b"; p525 p. 33, Gambling winnings, "If you’re in the trade or business of gambling, use Schedule C (Form 1040)")
Interpretation amb.2025.sli_ratio_places: Round the ratio half-up to three decimal places (the prorate atom with places 3 on sli.line8_figured). The owner's ruling. Three places is the least precision the text allows and the usual hand computation; the readings differ by at most a few cents of deduction (for example $167.50 against $166.67 on line 8 when line 6 is $1,000 and line 1 is $2,500).
 (i1040gi, p. 99, Student Loan Interest Deduction Worksheet—Schedule 1, Line 21, line 7)
Interpretation amb.2025.ss_wages: Use boxes 3 plus 7, summed across employers separately per person. Owner approved the Stage 2 proposal. iw2w3 p. 18 defines these boxes and their combined wage base; box 1 can exclude deferrals subject to Social Security. (iw2w3, p. 18, boxes 3 and 7; compare i1040gi p. 117, Schedule 3 line 11)
Interpretation amb.2025.savers_sep_contrib: The first (person.sav_simple_plan). The owner's decision (Stage 15 premise map, decision 2). Only elective deferrals to a SEP (code F, a salary reduction SEP) are named; a SEP contribution a self-employed person makes for themselves is a nonelective contribution. (f8880, p. 2, Line 2, "Elective deferrals (including designated Roth contributions under section 402A, if applicable) to a 401(k), 403(b), governmental 457(b), SEP, SIMPLE, or the federal TSP")
Interpretation amb.2025.ira_line7_cents: The first. The project's rule for products (amb.2025.multiplication_subcent). The readings differ only when the exact product is less than half a cent above a multiple of $10. (i1040gi, p. 98, IRA Deduction Worksheet—Continued, line 7, "If the result isn’t a multiple of $10, increase it to the next multiple of $10 (for example, increase $490.30 to $500)")
Scope: Form 1040 lines 1a–37 with the schedules and forms this tool implements and their refusal screens. Unsupported paths are refused with citations. No filing or transmission.
Rounding: amounts are exact cents; the return-wide whole-dollar rounding setting is deferred (see ambiguities/2025.yaml).
Estimated tax penalty: not computed; the IRS will figure it (see i1040gi, line 38).
This is a computation of published IRS forms and instructions; it is not tax or legal advice. Published instructions are not the law itself.
The user is responsible for the return. Not affiliated with or endorsed by the IRS.
